# The Ash Road — project log

Record a short entry at each meaningful integration, blocker resolution and playable release. State what actually shipped separately from source work. Keep detailed implementation notes in the linked reports.

Publish this log to [CA-The-destroyer/G-Ash_Road_ep1](https://github.com/CA-The-destroyer/G-Ash_Road_ep1) at meaningful build milestones. The repository currently hosts progress records. Licensed assets, saved campaigns, credentials and large build payloads remain local.


**Current playable master:** [Journey40](#2026-10-25--journey40-targeted-crash-repair-and-living-world-batch). Earlier failure/promotion records remain historical.

**Contents:** [Current milestone](#2026-10-25--journey40-targeted-crash-repair-and-living-world-batch) · [Methods and tools](#methods-and-tools) · [Recorded history](#recorded-project-history) · [Source commit ledger](#complete-recorded-source-commit-ledger)


## 2026-10-27 — Current Fighter transfer, saved visual proof and armor shader studies

Lab work only; Journey40, Main and private Mara remain unchanged. The current
A21 source import and native identity readback passed. Existing Epic connected
transfer and detail-parent operators restored 40 measured corner UVs and saved
three fresh diagnostic meshes without changing original source geometry or
imports. Fresh saved ten-view AMD capture passed. A body-backed pixel trace
identified the pale shoulder region as existing leather shading, not body
exposure. A single overlapping sleeve-fan center drifted about 2 mm during Walk.
An existing SkinWeightModifier diagnostic changed exactly its 18 wedges, kept the
other 130,302 vertex codes and all authored data exact, and saved a fresh mesh.
Root compared fresh NVIDIA oblique/Walk views: the specific sleeve dot is absent.
This narrow result does not approve the complete outfit, material finish or all
animations.

The male fitting donor now uses Epic's exported merge factory instead of
duplicating an incomplete renderless mesh. The previous duplication crash is
preserved. Actual NVIDIA readback matches all 60,816 saved body triangles,
full-body inclusion, the 342-bone reference and 27 DNA measurements. Saving
correctly stopped on a temporary body material. A new read-only actual inventory
passes and identifies six transient body textures without source payloads; the
parent material chain is persistent. Supported exact material/texture persistence
from the descriptor buffers is the next implementation, not a stock substitute.
No reusable male target is saved or accepted yet.

The mail/plate shader study now has all 13 diagnostic channels and a source
blend/UV layouts. Six finished maps were reused; seven remaining maps completed
on observed AMD HIP in 85.4 seconds. A temporary unwelded 117-to-1 bake proxy
passed exact positions, topology, corner normals, UVs, attributes, material routes
and original-source preservation. Two mapped light views exist. Normal gutters,
portable exports/readback, metal appearance and native/art acceptance remain
open. These are shader studies, not finished armor or runtime chain-link physics.

Methods/tools: installed Epic UE5.8.3 source and supported MetaHuman/Outfit APIs;
isolated Lab C++/Python; serial UnrealBuildTool/MSVC builds; NullRHI diagnostics;
actual AMD/NVIDIA saved-mesh captures; Steam Blender with bounded AMD HIP/CPU
jobs; source-control-point/corner-UV identities, exact bone-weight readback and
SHA256 preservation. Three bounded existing armor chats continue source,
material and target-dependency work under one integrating coordinator. No
gameplay regression or packaged replay is added for unchanged game systems.
Source preimages, failed attempts and generated evidence remain local. Only
this project log is published.

## 2026-10-26 — Actual Ranger production and universal visual fidelity

The owner rejected default-outfit demonstrations as a substitute for the Ranger
outfit. Work now targets the selected Ranger torsoR02, glovesR02, female
trousersR03 and bootsR02 on Mara. The first constructed chest is not accepted
finished art: the owner requires a serious art pass matching the approved coin
purse's leather and metal quality. Boots and trousers have approved shapes;
gloves are being corrected against their original layered reference.

**Universal acceptance rule:** every 3D component and its UV/material treatment
must reproduce its selected art almost exactly. Leather must read as leather,
straps must match scale and flow, and metal must read as metal. No component or
small-fitting exceptions. Technical import or fit success is not art approval.
The rule and repeatable armor-production direction are recorded in AGENTS.md.

**Methods:** parallel bounded component authoring, CPU headless Blender5.2.3,
reference/coordinate preservation, explicit exportable PBR channels and shared
Style48 metal treatment. A read-only purse comparison identifies concrete
geometry/UV/material differences. Root owns Unreal5.8.3 native Dataflow dressing,
C++ bridge, integration and focused native review. The already proven native
outfit workflow is reused; repeated default controls and another custom fitter
are excluded. Representative source fits will support native parametric resizing
instead of redesigning the same outfit for every wearer.

**Actual status:** Ranger authoring/art correction is in progress. The actual
Ranger has not yet passed native fitting or been activated in the game. The
current private Mara preview is a fixed single-body bake, not completed automatic
multi-body equipment fitting. Journey40 remains the playable master. No release,
asset upload or saved-campaign migration is implied by this entry.

Local work: tools/prepared/leather-armor25/mara-ranger-poc25/native-ranger26-a01;
root preparation/audit: tools/prepared/ranger-native26. Only this project log is
published; models, textures, licensed inputs, saves and build payloads stay local.


## 2026-10-25 — Native MetaHuman outfit control proves fitting, baking and sampled poses

The owner approved trying Epic's native clothing workflow. The isolated
CharacterLab now has newly constructed Cloth, Outfit and Wardrobe assets from a
known-good Epic garment, followed by genuine native fitting to the existing
female Mara descriptor's exported combined face/body and a baked skeletal mesh.
This is a stock shirt/shorts technical control, not the selected Ranger design.
Journey40 remains the playable master; current game Mara and campaign saves were
not replaced.

**Verified:** public Python Dataflow node/property/connection APIs; new native
skin-weight transfer and sized outfit; official combined-target export; actual
ResizeOutfitTemplate variables and execution; skeletal baking with342 bones,
13760 vertices and24989 triangles, no unweighted or invalid-bone vertices and
all normalized65535 weight sums. Fresh saved readback matches the output. Six
native rest/Idle/Walk sampled captures pass, with garment/body bone alignment and
45.3093cm observed walk change. Original source/template/descriptor files retain
their hashes. The capture's front/back filename labels are reversed; its explicit
visual-review correction identifies actual views without altering receipts.

**Methods/tools:** Epic documentation, installed public headers and real native
API probes; Unreal Python/Dataflow; a small editor-only C++ wrapper for Epic's
existing export, collection/topology/weight inspection and single-pose evaluation;
serial Unreal Editor jobs, explicit actual GPU vendor checks, native SceneCapture
readback and CPU Blender source inspection. Bounded parallel API/template reviews
supported the coordinator, who owned builds, asset saves and native execution.
No custom fitter/skinner, Engine patch or broad packaged regression suite was added.

**Corrections retained:** body-only input failed the combined-topology RBF check;
protected Bounds was not a usable Python read. An initial path audit misclassified
Windows separators. A native source count assertion compared the1973-triangle
Nanite fallback to the24989-triangle MeshDescription actually imported by
Dataflow; fresh source readback resolves the mismatch. Early capture callbacks
could reenter during native compilation and duplicate actors/lights, producing
D3D12 command-list exhaustion on both GPUs. Guarding the helper and explicitly
evaluating component poses yielded the passing focused check. Failed evidence
remains; the original source fit was not rerun just to repair an audit report.
Native inpaint transfer also used its closest-point fallback for some vertices;
that warning and the scope of visual checks remain explicit.

**Art boundary:** the saved pre-cleanup reconstruction already contains the
central jacket/sleeve holes. They predate DC512 cleanup. Ranger r09 and local
surface correction remain rejected; a sound static source must match selected
R02 coverage, sleeves/shoulders, continuous leather beneath the front X and the
legitimate rear X/diamond with plain rear waist belt. The native pipeline now
removes the need for another custom fitting algorithm, but does not repair bad
source geometry or establish likeness/art acceptance.

**Still open:** selected Ranger geometry/materials, actual Creator Wear UI and new
WI production assembly, continuous/all-pose fit, cloth simulation and runtime
wardrobe integration. Only sampled poses were checked. The render-only pilot is
baked for runtime use; no new release is claimed.

## 2026-10-25 — Ranger chest diagnostic measured and stopped during cleanup

Owner explicitly approved one monitored attempt after the coordinator explained
that its20GiB RAM/16GiB free-commit reservation was speculative. The one-job
wrapper gained an explicit owner-authorized monitored profile,4GiB entry reserve
and2GiB live emergency reserve; original conservative defaults remain for any
unapproved invocation. This does not lower the production2K image requirement:
the unchanged1254px master remains a diagnostic shape seed only.

Actual standard seed2401024 ran offline on the NVIDIA RTX4060Ti for~90seconds.
Shape generation reached remeshing/inner-layer cleanup for11,255,554 intermediate
quads. Actual worker lifetime peak commit was10,337MiB; total system free commit
fell to1,150MiB. The monitor stopped owned work. Supervisor exit1; no usable mesh
or result, no Blender fit/native import/Mara equipment change, and no retry.
Owned processes exited, the owned studio lock released, and postcheck NVIDIA
free VRAM returned to5,690MiB. No foreign process or system setting changed.

Methods/tools: existing pinned LocalMesh Engine and worker, exact offline cache,
atomic shared lock, no recursive lower-tier fallback,1200second bound, Python
Win32 memory counters and periodic nvidia-smi telemetry. A Windows venv redirector
initially made the wrapper's child-private counters irrelevant; a separate
parentage/command-verified monitor measured the actual inference child and its
lifetime peak. System commit includes other processes; this interrupted peak
is not a completed-run memory bound. Existing failed evidence is preserved.

The preceding final leather concept handoff is hash-verified:95 selected parts,
19 matching atlases,19 assembly guides;114 selected part/icon files match.
These are prepared concepts, not owner-approved3D or installed equipment.

Next geometry decision must constrain cleanup complexity or use a real larger
commit window, rather than lower the emergency reserve or silently repeat this
same expensive cleanup. Ranger→Mara fit/motion→one spoken facial response remains
the active order. Journey40/schema46 remains the playable master.

Outcome/evidence: `tools/prepared/mara-orchestration25/monitored-chest-attempt-a01.md`
and `.json`; complete local inventory: `RANGER-MONITORED25-CHANGED-FILES.md`/`.json`.

## 2026-10-25 — Main-character conversation face camera in the source preview

Owner liked the Mara face closeup and requested it for main-character
conversations. A local C++ camera helper now follows the existing dialogue RPC,
using explicit direct-Interact intent, configurable main-character IDs and an
optional actor tag. Initial IDs cover Mara, village messenger, wounded survivor
and courier. It blends into a collision-checked32degree face shot and back to
normal gameplay. Movement, damage, menus/travel, dismissal and camera loss or
takeover release the shot and its locally owned player hiding. Automatic shouts
without direct-talk intent do not initiate a closeup. No server quest, save,
combat or movement authority was changed.

Linked build a08 and focused witness `mara-preview25-5fcf48354d` pass. Real bound
F on existing Mara caused the production closeup, ordinary dialogue and saved
opening stage0→1. Natural dialogue expiry returned to the actual player camera
and92degree FOV after blending. The owned NVIDIA process exited normally0 and
inputs stayed immutable. Root inspected both actual conversation/return images.
A bounded independent source review caught and corrected ambient-trigger and
hidden-pawn cleanup issues. Other eligible NPCs/remote clients and every
interruption path are not separately rendered by this check.

The owner's Mara garment distortion remains a real rejected-art defect.
Hiding that garment exposed incomplete body coverage beneath the source outfit,
so the POC now retains the assembly's matching stock white shirt/shorts while
hiding only the corrupt medieval garment. The current source preview has
coherent temporary coverage; no medieval wardrobe fit is approved or repaired.
Offline garment trials remain rejected and unimported. The existing source
preview launcher readiness marker was refreshed; Journey40 remains the cooked
master, with no new release package and no owner-save modification.

Methods/tools: reused local camera/preview code and Epic camera/blend references;
narrow Unreal C++ hooks, sphere collision checks, linked UnrealBuildTool builds,
Python/PowerShell owned-process runner, actual GPU readback, normal input and
SQLite outcome observation, actual FOV/camera observations and screenshot review.
Build a05 compilation failure and the earlier a07 limited return observation

## 2026-10-22 — Fighter skill-tree source acceptance

### Integrated source; Journey21 remains the playable master

- The existing12-class/36-branch/144-node skill-tree foundation remains enabled. Its v1 catalogue IDs, exact JSON/header hashes, passive effects, points, ordered saved purchases, prerequisites and level-loss suspension are preserved. No allocation reset or free point grant was used.
- Fighter **Sure Strike**, existing `fighter.iron-measure.fork-a`, now adds a real level4 action: a correctly faced ordinary shield/dagger guard raised within0.28seconds of an eligible enemy melee hit (rank2:0.36seconds) earns a2second counter against that original attacker. The next normally admitted, paid melee swing consumes it, adding20% damage (rank2:30%) on valid contact. Normal reach, stamina, wear, recovery and damage authority remain; a miss or different target consumes the opportunity. This is not a general mainhand parry or a free extra attack. ## Methods and tools

This section describes the actual development workflow and its current limits.

| Area | Methods and tools in use |
| --- | --- |
| Game engine | Unreal Engine 5.8.3, C++20 gameplay modules, imported Blueprint assets and editable native data assets. Existing working systems are extended through focused modules. |
| Interface | Unreal Slate/SlateCore, shared ornate frame art and Crimson Text fonts, GUI menus and character-name entry. Screen-specific layout issues remain tracked separately. |
| Cooperative gameplay | Server-owned combat, encounter, inventory, quest, loot and persistence decisions; replicated actors/state and actual dedicated-server/client checks. Stable entity IDs and idempotent rewards prevent duplicate grants. |
| Saved world | SQLite-backed campaign services, schema versions, persistent encounter generations and saved quest/loot state. Save copies and retained packages support safe release transitions. |
| World construction | Original supplied map layouts, deterministic seeded placement, actual terrain/slope/water checks, capsule/route clearance and protected settlement/quest areas. Unreal Editor Python authoring writes bounded owned map packages with rollback archives. |
| Streaming and density | World Partition, nearby activation with distant logical records, bounded active AI populations, hierarchical instanced meshes, distance culling and retained mesh LODs. Current woodland work shares a wilderness limit of 12 active actors. |
| Navigation and encounters | Existing custom physical creature navigation and observed-target pursuit, finite search/return behavior, faction relationships and pack coordination. Actual movement/contact is checked separately from placement. |
| Combat and items | Existing custom RPG combat/ability services, graded enemy stats and damage dice, server-side physical projectiles, canonical inventory/loot and shared portable-object physics. Full crossbow, formations, universal prop conversion and ragdoll/dismemberment requests remain incomplete. |
| Rendering | DirectX 12/SM6 configuration with Lumen illumination/reflections, Temporal Super Resolution, virtual shadows and mesh distance fields. Hardware ray tracing is disabled in the current configuration. Materials, lighting, silhouettes and LODs are reviewed in actual rendered scenes. |
| Performance | Video quality controls, culling/LOD and bounded population budgets; one rendered test client at a time on the 8 GB target. Instance counts and a successful compile are not treated as proof of GPU performance. |
| Asset sources | User-supplied Fab/Epic/Quixel and other compatible asset packs, imported through Unreal tooling and existing project libraries. Source assets and provenance are preserved; owned collision/material derivatives are used for bounded fixes. |
| Asset creation | Blender, including headless asset preparation; Python for deterministic geometry/content generation and native import orchestration. |
| Art direction | Original medieval gothic world, relevant Dungeons & Dragons artwork as a standing visual reference, supplied town/world SVGs, forest photographs and entrance references. Skyrim-like exploration pacing is a design influence rather than a copied world layout. |
| Audio | Imported sound libraries, spatial attenuation and event cues, generated NPC dialogue through the local VoiceForge workflow, dedicated title music and menu feedback. Full sound coverage and natural performances remain ongoing work. |
| Automation scripts | Python and PowerShell for asset inventories, authoring, isolated verification, process ownership, freeze manifests, package ledgers and promotion. |
| Build tools | CMake for portable Core checks; MSVC, UnrealBuildTool and Unreal AutomationTool for native builds, cooking, staging and packaging. |
| Parallel development | Codex with named bounded agents, prepared changes and one integrating coordinator. Source ownership is divided; native builds, imports, map saves, cooks and promotions run serially. |
| Prior art | Repository inspection and official Epic documentation/samples guide integration. Existing project architecture is reused before introducing a new framework. |
| Verification | Focused Core contracts, real native floor/capsule queries, ordinary movement and interactions, rendered/cooked views and scoped server/client checks. Failed evidence is retained; source, built, verified and shipped are reported separately. |
| Versioning and releases | Local Git source checkpoints, exact file hashes and complete runtime/native freezes, immutable playtest directories, package readback before launcher promotion and current-plus-three release retention. |
| Project reporting | A dated GitHub project log records release milestones, design decisions, scoped checks, blockers and the next playable deliverable. Log publishing is separate from game-asset/build distribution. |

These practices describe the foundation and the work currently underway. The history below retains incomplete requests and prepared content rather than marking every planned system complete.

## Recorded project history

This chronology covers the retained prototype records, the original-world previews, Journey playtests 1–16, and the later development work awaiting a new package. It records what the evidence establishes, including limits and rejected candidates. A prepared feature, successful build, source commit, or screenshot is not a released playtest.

**History snapshot:** 20 October 2026 local time. The latest promoted playable build in the reviewed records is **0.21.0-preview12-playtest16**, runtime `8aaad40`, save schema 33. Current development has moved to schema 35; the next Journey package has not been promoted. Version suffixes matter: formal preview12 remained a delivery draft, while its separately named playtests were packaged and promoted.

Dates below use the recorded promotion or acceptance timestamp in UTC−05:00 where one exists. Git author dates in the appendix are source-work dates, not release dates. Earlier exact release dates and revisions are marked unknown where the retained record does not establish them. The local Git history begins at a verified 0.20.11 checkpoint on 16 October; earlier milestones are documented through retained delivery reports and receipts.

### Project direction and early decisions

The project began as a cooperative medieval fantasy RPG with a persistent, self-hosted, server-authoritative campaign. The initial target was one to five players, shared quest objectives and world changes, and individual characters, equipment, abilities, and rewards. Independent travel between regions and interiors was an explicit contract by 12 October 2026. Later bandit specifications proposed tuning for up to six participants; that proposal does not itself change the accepted multiplayer limit.

The exploration, woodland density, useful objects, and encounter rhythm of classic fantasy RPGs were references for an original world and story. The original vampire campaign became **The Ash Road**: Mara's camp, the attack on Harrowford, the cloth and survivor investigation, the healer and courier leads, the Ash Hound, and Briarwatch. The project is intended to support future stories through reusable quest, item, character, encounter, and persistence systems. Shouts, dragon souls, and a Dragonborn player role were excluded; this was not a general exclusion of fantasy dragon creatures.

Later art direction adopted D&D artwork, supplied forest photographs, town/world SVG layouts, and crypt/mine/dungeon entrance references. The recurring direction was natural, irregular woodland with mixed species and ages, deadwood, understory, and entrances grounded in hillsides. These references guide original work; they do not make the entire supplied atlas a finished playable world.

### Development after playtest16 — not yet a new release

The working source advances to save schema 35 on top of the recorded source history. The latest reviewed source checkpoint is `39448ca3`, which records retention work; later working-tree changes require an explicit freeze before packaging. The planned next master is Journey playtest17. No playtest17 package or promotion is claimed here.

- **Persistent bandit foundation:** deterministic sites and saved identities, a first camp with six defenders and two physical-projectile archers, alarm/evidence/prisoner interactions, camp cooking and shelter. Dedicated-server, two-remote-client, and late-join checks establish bounded projectile replication. The complete prisoner return/payment route and all tactical combinations remain unfinished.
- **Content library boundary:** twelve camp archetypes, sixteen quest scripts, and six boss definitions are content/catalogue foundations. They are not sixteen delivered, independently playable camp quests or six accepted boss encounters. The broader quest graph remains held development, and full crossbow, faction, tactical, and repopulation coverage is not complete.
- **Woodland population:** 48 surveyed habitat sites, 32 neutral and 16 hostile, with 82 potential occupants under a shared twelve-active-AI budget; four three-wolf packs and finite observed hunting/fleeing behavior. Targeted ordinary deer/wolf/ghoul behavior was observed. This does not establish full regional ecology, town raids, or all multiplayer habitat combinations.
- **Forest concealment and camp presentation:** a saved outer camp pass with 300 trees, 1,800 grass, 120 understory, eight deadwood and six rocks; six focused habitat groves with 192 trees, 288 understory and eighteen deadwood; an inner camp pass with fourteen trees, 500 grass and fifty understory. Actual author/descriptor/route guards exist, while final visual acceptance remains a separate gate. These six groves do not make all 48 sites dense woodland.
- **Crypt reliability:** fall/checkpoint safeguards and focused wall/stair-corner traversal diagnosis are active. A recorded lower-floor walk is not evidence that every enemy and player route is safe. Further use of the installed Medieval Dungeon kit is deferred expansion input while the current blocker is investigated.
- **Meadow work:** the original prairie candidate failed before placement. The later four-meadow pass saved75,050 plants in409 spatial groups after its geometry/new-instance budget gates passed. Rendered and GPU acceptance remain open; the current-status section above records the latest mastering gate.
- **Storage:** current plus three prior releases is the adopted retention policy. Saved campaigns and useful diagnostics are retained separately; the cleanup report records about 179.7 GB recovered. This is not permission to delete active user saves or terminate an open process.

### Request and design-decision history retained in the backlog

The feature consolidation reviewed 64 registered feature areas and a 21-area backlog on 20 October. It proposed four ongoing workstreams—campaign/co-op/dungeon; player systems/interface/crafting/loot; combat/NPC simulation/equipment; world/characters/art/audio—with one debug queue and coordinated integration. The proposed 53 additional chat archives had **not** been executed. Consolidation reduces navigation overhead; it does not close unfinished requirements.

| Backlog area | Requests, decisions, and present delivery boundary |
|---|---|
| 1. Vampire campaign and side quests | Build an original consequential vampire story and reusable story framework. Keep deliberate progression, meaningful craft/fetch rewards, branching outcomes, and follow-up clues. Several opening/side stories ship; the full campaign does not. |
| 2. Briarwatch dungeon | Expand beyond a door and two rooms into multiple meaningful floors, encounters, clues, rewards, and bosses. Playtest13 delivers three lower floors/eighteen rooms; traversal, encounter depth, puzzles, and further dressing remain. |
| 3. Enemy AI and wilderness danger | Longer credible pursuit; smooth movement; monsters that hunt neutrals, bandits, and eventually settlements; roaming packs and dangerous nights. Human and goblin camps stay separate. Difficulty should come from real terrain, cover, alarms, commanders, and finite reinforcements rather than hidden stat compensation. Current work is a bounded foundation. |
| 4. Audio, voices, and music | Material-specific chopping/footsteps, angry rat sounds, creature movement/idles, nearly every usable asset sounding appropriately, spatial dialogue blended with ambience, terrified aftermath lines, and distinct menu music/cues. Temporary candidate voices were authorized; less robotic Mara/messenger performances were then requested. Mara replacement takes ship in playtest12 and expressive messenger takes in playtest15; subjective listening, full cast, lip sync, and complete event coverage remain. |
| 5. Reliability and debug triage | Route Debug-tagged reports to a dedicated queue while main production continues. Retain reports about jittering animals, trapped doors, crypt traversal, large dark geometry, crash stability, UI text, armor, shields, and axe orientation. Targeted fixes do not close every related global issue. |
| 6. World, settlements, and terrain | Use original map layouts, broad real travel distances, grounded hillsides, banks/cliffs, chaotic varied forests, understory, deadwood, and usable town services/interiors. Aftermath should include dead villagers, graves, pyres, blood and selected fires. Full atlas coverage and every settlement remain future work. |
| 7. Character progression and opening equipment | Start in Mara's borrowed tunic with low initial equipment. The Peltsmith supplies lesser recovered hide/leather, never free class armor; earn a class weapon through skeever work. Preserve point buy, individual XP, level healing/audio, and future balance review. |
| 8. Branching class skill trees | Priority request for advanced branches, meaningful skill points, unique class playstyles, and high-quality icons. Saved purchasing and passive consumers ship; advanced depth and final icon coverage remain. |
| 9. Combat and progressive equipment | Use supplied weapon assets for recognizable axes, shields, staves, monk equipment and progression. Correct grips/orientation and support a relaxed posture. Universal weapon integration and distinct damage-type behavior are not complete. |
| 10. Character appearance | Modern detailed faces and skin, wider face choices/sliders, joined necks/heads/hands, individual armor identity, and cape fit. Existing presets/tints are foundations; held detailed-face work is not a delivered character creator upgrade. Natural ragdolls and dismemberment remain requested scope. |
| 11. Interface and message history | Ornate wood/brass framing and readable fonts across windows, scaled text, working inventory controls, a bottom EverQuest-like log, newest-first messages, expiry after about ten seconds, scrolling and journal history. Several stages ship; clipping and all-resolution review remain. |
| 12. Loot, containers, and economy | Individual useful food, books, coins, equipment and jewelry; subtle unlooted glow; keys/lockpicking, condition, depth-appropriate rewards and a fence for stolen goods. Finite loot/physics coverage does not satisfy universal ownership/theft/economy behavior. |
| 13. Loose-object physics | Small objects should have mass, gravity, collision, carry/drag/drop, and persistent resting poses globally. The 732-placement pass is real finite coverage; not every decorative mesh is portable, lootable, or safe in every carry/concurrency case. |
| 14. Crafting, alchemy, and gathering | Larger obvious sticks/grass, registered trees falling to an axe then disappearing after looting, logs into sticks, creative discoverable recipes such as rat teeth for arrow points, ingredient/pouch support, and all usable fires/pots cooking. Some recipes and tree coverage ship; universal coverage remains. |
| 15. Living NPCs and services | Natural turning to speakers, messengers running/shouting into scenes, work/social routines and camp interactions rather than patrol alone; terrified villagers and scared survivor motions; accessible healer/trader/stations. Messenger staging is player-validated; broad schedules, shops, and social behavior remain incomplete. |
| 16. Stealth, crime, guards, and factions | Pickpocketing, witnessed theft, evasion, fence trading, town guards and lasting faction consequences. Four guards and new camp foundations exist; the full crime/faction simulation does not. |
| 17. Co-op, death, rescue, and checkpoints | Preserve shared world state with independent travel; nearby beds/refuges/checkpoints and rescue. Party-cohesion pressure outside safe areas should preserve solo, loading, and rescue exceptions. A lone-wolf ambush proposal is not a finished general system. |
| 18. Maps, navigation, and travel | M opens the map; refine broad location to precise position/facing, quest beacons, signs to crypt/towns, and hidden DM warp for testing. Full atlas routes, mounts, climbing/swimming and finished DM roles remain later work. |
| 19. Magic identity | Each magic user/class should have distinctive spell art/effects; expand free aiming and meaningful environmental reactions. Mana, ranks, and talent consumers exist; the full spell/effects library does not. |
| 20. Loading, video, and accessibility | Title/menu flow, GUI hero naming, New/Save/Load/Options, loading tips, manual quality presets, shadows/render distance, safe rollback and lower-GPU support. Automatic adaptive GPU tuning remains prepared/planned rather than accepted runtime behavior. |
| 21. Longer-term systems | Preserve requirements for hosting, property/housing, injuries, faction arcs and transformations without representing them as current delivery. Keep excluded player-role features excluded. |

The user repeatedly authorized parallel agents for bounded tasks and preferred more content with targeted verification over broad repeated retesting. Serial native build/import/cook ownership, a reviewable freeze, meaningful multiplayer/persistence gates, and explicit promotion remain necessary to distinguish useful preparation from a playable master.

### How to read the evidence

The release reports and promotion receipts establish runtime identity and packaging; native readbacks establish saved map/content facts; actual gameplay probes establish only their measured routes and interactions. Unit/core simulations, editor renders, packaged screenshots, remote clients, user playtests, and subjective listening are different evidence types. Known failures and held work stay visible rather than being converted into completion claims.

The companion source index contains reviewed relative source paths and SHA-256 hashes, release identity gaps, and the complete reachable local source commit metadata. Public prose contains no local user paths, credentials, private campaign identities, or asset payloads. Requested omitted implementation topics are excluded from the public narrative and ledger.

### Complete recorded source commit ledger

This is the complete reachable local source history across all recorded branches at the audit snapshot, after the requested topic/privacy filter. Parallel branches and cherry-picked changes have distinct hashes and therefore remain separate entries. These commits are not all shipped releases, and their presence here does not imply that this public log repository contains their source trees. Author timestamps are preserved; they can differ from commit or promotion time.
